Appendix G
Appendix to Section
7
– Soundness of the Hoare Logics

G.1.
Expectations

Axiom G.1.

We require a sound logic of assertions (
M\vdash A
), and a sound Hoare logic ,
i.e.
that for all
M
,
A
,
A^{\prime}
,
stmt
:

M\vdash A\ \ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ \forall\sigma.[\ M,\sigma\models A\ ]
.

M\ \vdash_{ul}\ \{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\ \ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ M\models\{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}

The expectation that
M\ \vdash_{ul}\ \{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}
is sound is not onerous:
since the assertions
A
and
A^{\prime}
do not talk about protection, many Hoare logics from the literature could be taken.

On the other hand, in the logic
M\vdash A
we want to allow the assertion
A
to talk about protection.
Since protection is a novel concept, the literature offers no such logics.
Nevertheless, such a logic can be constructed by extending and underlying assertion logic
M\vdash_{ul}A
which does not talk about protection.
We show such an extension in Fig
18
.

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{c}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ {{\mbox{[\sc{Ext-1}]}}}\\
M\vdash_{ul}A\\
\hline\cr M\vdash_{ul}A\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{lcl}\begin{array}[]{c}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ {{\mbox{[\sc{Ext-2}]}}}\\
M\vdash A\rightarrow A^{\prime}\par\\
\hline\cr M\vdash(A\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mathit{e}^{\prime})\rightarrow(A^{\prime}\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mathit{e}^{\prime})\end{array}&&\begin{array}[]{c}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ {{\mbox{[\sc{Ext-3}]}}}\\
M\vdash A\rightarrow A^{\prime}\par\\
\hline\cr M\vdash(A\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle{)\rightarrow(A^{\prime}\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle})\end{array}\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{c}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ {{\mbox{[\sc{Ext-4}]}}}\\
\\
\hline\cr M\vdash{((A_{1}\vee A_{2})\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mathit{e}^{\prime}))\leftrightarrow((A_{1}\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mathit{e}^{\prime})\vee(A_{2}\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mathit{e}^{\prime}))}\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{c}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ {{\mbox{[\sc{Ext-5}]}}}\\
\\
\hline\cr M\vdash{((A_{1}\vee A_{2})\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle)\leftrightarrow((A_{1}\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle)\vee(A_{2}\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle))}\end{array}\end{array}

Figure 18
.
From
M\vdash_{ul}A
to
M\vdash A

The extension shown in in Fig.
18
preserves soundness of the logic:

Lemma G.2.

Assume a logic
\vdash_{ul}
, such that

M\vdash_{ul}A\ \ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ \forall\sigma.[\ M,\sigma\models A\ ]
.

Extend this logic according to the rules in Fig.
18
and in Fig
17
, and obtain
M\vdash A
. Then, we have:

M\vdash A\ \ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ \forall\sigma.[\ M,\sigma\models A\ ]
.

Proof.

By induction over the derivation that
M\vdash A
.
∎

Note that the rules in in Fig.
18
allow the derivation of
M\vdash A
,
for which
{Stb^{+}(A)}
does not hold –
e.g.
we can derive
M\vdash\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle\rightarrow\langle\!\!\langle\mathit{e}\rangle\!\!\rangle
through application of rule
Ext-3
.
However, this does not affect soundness of our logic –
{Stb^{+}(\_)}
is required only in specifications.

G.2.
Deep satisfaction of assertions

Definition G.3.

For a state
\sigma
, and a number
i\in\mathbb{N}
with
i\leq|\sigma|
, module
M
, and assertions
A
,
A^{\prime}
we define:

•

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A}\ \ \ \triangleq\ \ \ k\leq|\sigma|\ \wedge\ \forall i\!\in\![k...|\sigma|].[\ M,{\sigma[i]}\models A[\overline{{{\lfloor z\rfloor_{\sigma}}/z}}]\ ]\ \ \mbox{where}\ \ \overline{z}={\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A).

Remember the definition of
\sigma[k]
, which returns a new state whose top frame is the
k
-th frame from
\sigma
. Namely,
(\phi_{1}...\phi_{i}...\phi_{n},\chi)[i]\ \ \ \triangleq\ \ \ (\phi_{1}...\phi_{i},\chi)

Lemma G.4.

For a states
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
, numbers
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
, assertions
A
,
A^{\prime}
, frame
\phi
and variables
\overline{z}
,
\overline{u}
:

(1)

M,\sigma,{|\sigma|}\models{A}\ \ \Longleftrightarrow\ \ M,\sigma\models A\

(2)

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A}\ \wedge\ k\leq k^{\prime}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ M,\sigma,{k^{\prime}}\models{A}

(3)

M,\sigma\models A\ \wedge\ {Stbl(A)}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \forall k\leq|\sigma|.[\ M,\sigma,{k}\models{A}\ ]

(4)

M\models A\rightarrow A^{\prime}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \forall\sigma.\forall k\leq{|\sigma|}.[\ M,\sigma,{k}\models{A}\ \Longrightarrow\ M,\sigma,{k}\models{A}^{\prime}\ ]

Proof Sketch

(1)

By unfolding and folding the definitions.

(2)

By unfolding and folding the definitions.

(3)

By induction on the definition of
{Stbl(\_)}
.

(4)

By contradiction: Assume that there exists a
\sigma
, and a
k\leq|\sigma|
, such that

\hskip 56.9055ptM,\sigma,{k}\models{A}
and
\neg(M,\sigma,{k}\models{A}^{\prime})

The above implies that

\hskip 56.9055pt\forall i\geq k.[\ \ M,{\sigma[i]}\models A[\overline{{{\lfloor z\rfloor_{\sigma}}/z}}]\ \ ]
, and

\hskip 56.9055pt\exists j\geq k.[\ \ M,{\sigma[j]}\not\models A^{\prime}[\overline{{{\lfloor z\rfloor_{\sigma}}/z}}]\ \ ]
,

where
\overline{z}\triangleq{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A)\cup{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A^{\prime})
.

Take
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\triangleq\sigma[j]
. Then we have that

\hskip 56.9055ptM,\sigma^{\prime\prime}\models A[\overline{{{\lfloor z\rfloor_{\sigma}}/z}}]
, and
M,\sigma^{\prime\prime}\not\models A^{\prime}[\overline{{{\lfloor z\rfloor_{\sigma}}/z}}]
.

This contradicts
M\models A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
.

End Proof Sketch

Finally, the following lemma allows us to combine shallow and Deep satisfaction:

Lemma G.5.

For states
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
, frame
\phi
such that
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\,{\triangledown}\,\phi
, and for
assertion
A
, such that
fv(A)=\emptyset
:

•

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A}\ \wedge\ M,\sigma^{\prime}\models A\ \ \ \Longleftrightarrow\ \ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A}

Proof.

By structural induction on
A
, and unfolding/folding the definitions. Using also lemma
G.19
from later.
∎

G.3.
Shallow and Deep Semantics of Hoare tuples

Another example demonstrating that assertions at the end of a method execution might not hold after the call:

Example G.6 (
Stb^{+}
not always preserved by Method Return).

Assume state
\sigma_{a}
, such that
\lfloor\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}\rfloor_{\sigma_{a}}=o_{1}
,
\lfloor\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.f\rfloor_{\sigma}=o_{2}
,
\lfloor x\rfloor_{\sigma}=o_{3}
,
\lfloor x.f\rfloor_{\sigma}=o_{2}
,
and
\lfloor x.g\rfloor_{\sigma}=o_{4}
, where
o_{2}
is external and all other objects are internal.
We then have
..,\sigma_{a}\models\langle\!\!\langle o_{4}\rangle\!\!\rangle
.
Assume the continuation of
\sigma_{a}
consists of a method
x.m()
. Then,
upon entry to that method, when we push the new frame, we have state
\sigma_{b}
, which also satisfies
..,\sigma_{b}\models\langle\!\!\langle o_{4}\rangle\!\!\rangle
.
Assume the body of
m
is
\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.f.m1(\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.g);\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.f:=\mbox{{\texttt{this}}};\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.g:=\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}
, and the external method
m1
stores in the
receiver a reference to the argument.
Then, at the end of method execution, and before popping the stack, we have state
\sigma_{c}
, which also satisfies
..,\sigma_{c}\models\langle\!\!\langle o_{4}\rangle\!\!\rangle
.
However, after we pop the stack, we obtain
\sigma_{d}
, for which
..,\sigma_{d}\not\models\langle\!\!\langle o_{4}\rangle\!\!\rangle
.

Definition G.7 (Deep Satisfaction of Quadruples by States).

For modules
\overline{M}
,
M
, state
\sigma
,
and assertions
A
,
A^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime\prime}

•

{\overline{M};M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \,\{\,A\,\}\,\sigma\,\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\parallel\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}}\ \ \triangleq\ \

\hskip 14.22636pt\forall k,\overline{z},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime}.[M,\sigma,{k}\models{A}\ \ \Longrightarrow

\hskip 128.0374pt[\ {M\!\cdot\!\overline{M};\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma^{\prime}}\ \Rightarrow\ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{{{A^{\prime}}}}\ ]\ \wedge

\hskip 128.0374pt[\ {M\!\cdot\!\overline{M};\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\ \Rightarrow\ M,\sigma^{\prime\prime},{k}\models{(\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[{{\overline{\lfloor z\rfloor_{\sigma}/z}}}])}\ ]

\hskip 65.44142pt\ \ \ \ ]

\hskip 65.44142pt\ \ \ \ \mbox{where }{{\overline{z}={\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A)}}

Lemma G.8.

For all
M
,
\overline{M}

A
,
A^{\prime}
,
A^{\prime\prime}
and
\sigma
:

•

{\overline{M};M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \,\{\,A\,\}\,\sigma\,\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\parallel\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}}\ \Longrightarrow\ \ {\overline{M};M\,\models\,\{\,A\,\}\,\sigma\,\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\parallel\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}}

We define the
meaning
of our Hoare triples,
\{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}
, in the usual way,
i.e.
that execution of
stmt
in a state that satisfies
A
leads to a state which satisfies
A^{\prime}
.
In addition to that, Hoare quadruples,
\{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}
, promise that any external future states scoped by
\sigma
will satisfy
A^{\prime\prime}
.
We give both a weak and a shallow version of the semantics

Definition G.9 (Deep Semantics of Hoare triples).

For modules
M
, and assertions
A
,
A^{\prime}
we define:

•

M\models\{\,A\,\}\ stmt\ \{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\ \ \ \triangleq

\ \ \ \forall\overline{M}.\forall\sigma.[\ \ \sigma.\mbox{{\texttt{cont}}}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}stmt\ \Longrightarrow\ {\overline{M};M\,\models\,\{\,\ A\ \,\}\,\sigma\,\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\parallel\,\{\,true\,\}}\ \ ]

•

M\models\{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}\ \ \ \triangleq

\ \ \ \forall\overline{M}.\forall\sigma.[\ \ \sigma.\mbox{{\texttt{cont}}}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}stmt\ \Longrightarrow\ {\overline{M};M\,\models\,\{\,\ A\ \,\}\,\sigma\,\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\parallel\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}}\ \ ]

•

M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \{\,A\,\}\ stmt\ \{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\ \ \ \triangleq

\ \ \ \forall\overline{M}.\forall\sigma.[\ \ \sigma.\mbox{{\texttt{cont}}}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}stmt\ \Longrightarrow\ {\overline{M};M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \,\{\,\ A\ \,\}\,\sigma\,\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\parallel\,\{\,true\,\}}\ \ ]

•

M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}\ \ \ \triangleq

\ \ \ \forall\overline{M}.\forall\sigma.[\ \ \sigma.\mbox{{\texttt{cont}}}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}stmt\ \Longrightarrow\ {\overline{M};M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \,\{\,\ A\ \,\}\,\sigma\,\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\parallel\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}}\ \ ]

Lemma G.10 (Deep vs Shallow Semantics of Quadruples).

For all
M
,
A
,
A^{\prime}
, and
stmt
:

•

M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ M\models\{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}

G.4.
Deep satisfaction of specifications

We now give a Deep meaning to specifications:

Definition G.11 (Deep Semantics of Specifications).

We define
M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ {S}
by cases:

(1)

M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \ \rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{{$\mathbb{A}\!\!$}}{\overline{x:C}}.{{\{}}{A}{{\}}}\ \ \ \triangleq\ \ \ \forall\sigma.[\ M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \{\,\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}\wedge\overline{x:C}\wedge A\,\}\ \sigma\{\,A\,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,A\,\}\ ]

(2)

M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \{\,A_{1}\,\}\,p\ D\!::\!m(\overline{y:D})\,\{\,A_{2}\,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{A_{3}\}\ \ \ \ \triangleq\ \ \

\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \forall y_{0},\overline{y},\sigma[\ \ \ \sigma\mbox{{\texttt{cont}}}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}{u:=y_{0}.m(y_{1},..y_{n})}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \{\,A_{1}^{\prime}\,\}\ \sigma\{\,A_{2}^{\prime}\,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,A_{3}^{\prime}\,\}\ \ \ ]

where

\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ A_{1}^{\prime}\triangleq y_{0}:D,{\overline{y:D}}\wedge A[y_{0}/\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}],\ \ A_{2}^{\prime}\triangleq A_{2}[u/res,y_{0}/\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}],\ \ A_{3}^{\prime}\triangleq A_{3}[y_{0}/\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}]

(3)

M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ S\,\wedge\,S^{\prime}

\triangleq

M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ S\ \wedge\ M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ S^{\prime}

Lemma G.12 (Deep vs Shallow Semantics of Quadruples).

For all
M
,
S
:

•

M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ S\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ M\models S

G.5.
Soundness of the Hoare Triples Logic

Auxiliary Lemma G.13.

For any module
M
, assertions
A
,
A^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime\prime}
, such that
{Stb^{+}(A)}
, and
{Stb^{+}(A^{\prime})}
, and a statement
stmt
which does not contain any method calls:

M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ M\models\!\!\!\!_{{}_{deep}}\ \{\,A\,\}\ stmt\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}

G.5.1.
Lemmas about protection

Definition G.14.

LocRchbl(\sigma,k)\ \ \ \triangleq\ \ \ \{\alpha\mid\exists i.[\ k\leq i\leq 0pt\sigma\ \wedge\ \alpha\in LocRchbl(\sigma[i])\ ]

Lemma
G.15
guarantees that program execution reduces the locally reachable objects, unless it allocates new ones.
That is, any objects locally reachable in the
k
-th frame of the new state (
\sigma^{\prime}
), are either new, or were locally reachable in the
k
-th frame of the previous state (
\sigma
).

Lemma G.15.

For all
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
, and
\alpha
, where
\models\sigma
, and where
k\leq|\sigma|
:

•

\overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma}\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ LocRchbl(\sigma^{\prime},k)\cap\sigma\subseteq\ LocRchbl(\sigma,k)

•

\overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ LocRchbl(\sigma^{\prime},k)\cap\sigma\subseteq\ LocRchbl(\sigma,k)

Proof.

•

If the step is a method call, then the assertion follows by construction.
If the steps is a local execution in a method, we proceed by case analysis. If it is an assignment to a local variable, then

\forall k.[\ LocRchbl(\sigma^{\prime},k)=LocRchbl(\sigma,k)\ ]
.
If the step is the creation of a new object, then the assertion holds by construction.
If it it is a field assignment, say,
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[\alpha_{1},f\mapsto\alpha_{2}]
, then we have that

\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\ LocRchbl(\sigma,|\sigma|)
.
And therefore, by Lemma
B.3
, we also have that
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\ LocRchbl(\sigma,k)

All locally reachable objects in
\sigma^{\prime}
were either already reachable in
\sigma
or reachable through
\alpha_{2}
,
Therefore, we also have that
LocRchbl(\sigma^{\prime},k)\subseteq LocRchbl(\sigma,k)

And by definition of
\_;\,{\_}\rightsquigarrow\_
, it is not a method return.

•

By induction on the number of steps in
\overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime}
.
For the steps that correspond to method calls, the assertion follows by construction.
For the steps that correspond to local execution in a method, the assertion follows from the bullet above.
For the steps that correspond to method returns, the assertion follows by lemma
B.3
.

∎

Lemma
G.16
guarantees that any change to the contents of an external object can only happen during execution of an external method.

Lemma G.16.

For all
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
:

•

\overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma}\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\ \wedge\ \sigma\models\alpha:\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}\ \wedge\ {\lfloor\alpha.f\rfloor_{\sigma}}\neq{\lfloor\alpha.f\rfloor_{\sigma^{\prime}}}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}

Proof.

Through inspection of the operational semantics in Fig.
5
, and in particular rule
Write
.
∎

Lemma
G.17
guarantees that internal code which does not include method calls preserves absolute protection.
It is used in the proof of soundness of the inference rule
Prot-1
.

Lemma G.17.

For all
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
, and
\alpha
:

•

M,\sigma,{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}\,\wedge\,M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{intl}}}\,\wedge\,\sigma.\mbox{{\texttt{cont}}}\mbox{ contains no method calls }\ \wedge\ \overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma}\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}

•

M,\sigma,{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}\ \wedge\ M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{intl}}}\ \wedge\ \sigma.\mbox{{\texttt{cont}}}\mbox{ contains no method calls }\ \wedge\ \overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}

Proof.

•

Because
\sigma.\mbox{{\texttt{cont}}}
contains no method calls, we also have that
|\sigma^{\prime}|=|\sigma|
. Let us take

m=|\sigma|
.

We continue by contradiction.
Assume that
M,\sigma,{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}
and
M,\sigma,{k}\not\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}

Then:

(*)
\forall f.\forall i\in[k..m].\forall\alpha_{o}\in LocRchbl(\sigma,i).[\ M,\sigma\models\alpha_{o}:\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}\Rightarrow\lfloor\alpha_{o}.f\rfloor_{\sigma}\neq\alpha\ \wedge\alpha_{o}\neq\alpha\ ]
.

(**)
\exists f.\exists j\in[k..m].\exists\alpha_{o}\in LocRchbl(\sigma^{\prime},j).[\ M,\sigma^{\prime}\models\alpha_{o}:\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}\wedge\lfloor\alpha_{o}.f\rfloor_{\sigma^{\prime}}=\alpha\ \vee\alpha_{o}=\alpha\ ]

We proceed by cases

:

1st Case
\alpha_{o}\notin\sigma
,
i.e.

\alpha_{o}
is a new object.
Then, by our operational semantics, it cannot have a field pointing to an already existing object (
\alpha
), nor can it be equal with
\alpha
. Contradiction.

:

2nd Case
\alpha_{o}\in\sigma
. Then, by Lemma
G.15
, we obtain that
\alpha_{o}\in LocRchbl(\sigma,j)
.
Therefore, using (*), we obtain that
\lfloor\alpha_{o}.f\rfloor_{\sigma}\neq\alpha
, and therefore

\lfloor\alpha_{o}.f\rfloor_{\sigma}\neq\lfloor\alpha_{o}.f\rfloor_{\sigma^{\prime}}
.
By lemma
G.16
, we obtain
M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}
. Contradiction!

•

By induction on the number of steps, and using the bullet above.

∎

Lemma G.18.

For all
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
, and
\alpha
:

•

M,\sigma\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha_{o}}\ \wedge\ \sigma.\mbox{{\texttt{heap}}}=\sigma^{\prime}.\mbox{{\texttt{heap}}}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ M,\sigma^{\prime}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha_{o}}

By unfolding and folding the definitions.
∎

M,\sigma\models\mathit{e}:\mbox{{\texttt{intl*}}}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \forall\overline{f}.[\ M,\sigma\models\mathit{e}.\overline{f}:\mbox{{\texttt{intl}}}\ ]

G.6.
Well-founded ordering

Definition G.22.

For a module
M
, and modules
\overline{M}
, we define a measure,
[\!\![\,A,\sigma,A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}]\!\!]\,_{M,\overline{M}}
, and based on it, a well founded ordering
(A_{1},\sigma_{1},A_{2},A_{3})\ll_{M,\overline{M}}(A_{4},\sigma_{2},A_{5},A_{6})

as follows:

•

[\!\![\,A,\sigma,A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}]\!\!]\,_{M,\overline{M}}\ \ \triangleq\ \ (m,n)
, where

–

m
is the minimal number of execution steps so that
M\cdot\overline{M};\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma^{\prime}
for some
\sigma^{\prime}
, and
\infty
otherwise.

–

n
is minimal depth of all proofs of
M\vdash\{\,A\,\}\ \sigma.\mbox{{\texttt{cont}}}\{\,A^{\prime}\,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,A^{\prime\prime}\,\}
.

•

(m,n)\ll(m^{\prime},n^{\prime})

\triangleq

m<m^{\prime}\vee(m=m^{\prime}\wedge n<n^{\prime})
.

•

(A_{1},\sigma_{1},A_{2},A_{3})\ll_{M,\overline{M}}(A_{4},\sigma_{2},A_{5},A_{6})

\triangleq

[\!\![\,A_{1},\sigma_{1},A_{2},A_{3}]\!\!]\,_{M,\overline{M}}\ll[\!\![\,A_{4},\sigma_{2},A_{5}.A_{6}]\!\!]\,_{M,\overline{M}}

Lemma G.23.

For any modules
M
and
\overline{M}
, the relation
\_\ll_{M,\overline{M}}\_
is well-founded.

G.7.
Public States, properties of executions consisting of several steps

We t define a state to be public, if the currently executing method is public.

Definition G.24.

We use the form

M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{pub}}}
to express that the currently executing method is public.
16
16

16

This can be done by looking in the caller’s frame – ie the one right under the topmost frame – the class of the current receiver and the name of the currently executing method, and then looking up the method definition in the module
M
; if not defined there, then it is not
public
.

Note that
pub
is not part of the assertion language.

Auxiliary Lemma G.25 (Enclosed Terminating Executions).

For modules
\overline{M}
, states
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
,
\sigma_{1}
:

•

\overline{M};\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma^{\prime}\ \wedge\ \overline{M};\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma_{1}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ \exists\sigma_{2}.[\ \ \overline{M};\,{\sigma_{1}}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma_{2}\ \wedge\ (\overline{M},\sigma);\,{\sigma_{2}}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime}\ \ ]

Auxiliary Lemma G.26 (Executing sequences).

For modules
\overline{M}
, statements
s_{1}
,
s_{2}
, states
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
,
\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}
:

•

\sigma.\texttt{cont}=s_{1};s_{2}\ \ \wedge\ \ \overline{M};\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \wedge\ \ \overline{M};\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}\

\Longrightarrow

\exists\sigma^{\prime\prime}.[\ \ \ \ \ \overline{M};\,{\sigma[\texttt{cont}\mapsto s_{1}]}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma^{\prime\prime}\ \wedge\ \overline{M};\,{\sigma^{\prime\prime}[\texttt{cont}\mapsto s_{2}]}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma^{\prime}\ \wedge

\hskip 34.14322pt[\ \ \overline{M};\,{\sigma[\texttt{cont}\mapsto s_{1}]}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}\ \vee\ \overline{M};\,{\sigma^{\prime\prime}[\texttt{cont}\mapsto s_{2}]}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\ ]\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ ]

G.8.
Summarised Executions

We repeat the two diagrams given in §
7
.

The diagram opposite shows such an execution:

\overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma_{2}}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma_{30}
consists of 4 calls to external objects,
and 3 calls to internal objects.
The calls to external objects are from
\sigma_{2}
to
\sigma_{3}
, from
\sigma_{3}
to
\sigma_{4}
, from
\sigma_{9}
to
\sigma_{10}
,
and from
\sigma_{16}
to
\sigma_{17}
.
The calls to internal objects are from
\sigma_{5}
to
\sigma_{6}
, rom
\sigma_{7}
to
\sigma_{8}
, and from
\sigma_{21}
to
\sigma_{23}
.

In terms of our example, we want to summarise the execution of the two “outer” internal, public methods into the
“large” steps
\sigma_{6}
to
\sigma_{19}
and
\sigma_{23}
to
\sigma_{24}
.
And are not concerned with the states reached from these two public method executions.

In order to express such summaries, Def.
G.27
introduces the following concepts:

•

{(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma^{\prime}}
execution from
\sigma
to
\sigma^{\prime}
scoped by
\sigma_{sc}
, involving external states only.

•

{(\overline{M}\cdot M);\sigma{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{p}\,\sigma^{\prime}{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{1}\,}}

{\sigma}
is an external state calling an internal public method, and

\sigma^{\prime}
is the state after return from the public method, and

\sigma_{1}
is the first state upon entry to the public method.

Continuing with our example, we have the following execution summaries:

(1)

{(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{3}});\sigma_{3}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma_{5}}
Purely external execution from
\sigma_{3}
to
\sigma_{5}
, scoped by
\sigma_{3}
.

(2)

{(\overline{M}\cdot M);\sigma_{5}{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{p}\,\sigma_{20}{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{6}\,}}
. Public method call from external state
\sigma_{5}
into nternal state
\sigma_{6}
returning to
\sigma_{20}
.
Note that this summarises two internal method executions (
\sigma_{6}-\sigma_{19}
, and
\sigma_{8}-\sigma_{14}
),
and two external method executions (
\sigma_{6}-\sigma_{19}
, and
\sigma_{8}-\sigma_{14}
).

(3)

{(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{3}});\sigma_{20}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma_{21}}
.

(4)

{(\overline{M}\cdot M);\sigma_{21}{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{p}\,\sigma_{25}{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{23}\,}}
. Public method call from external state
{\sigma_{21}}
into internal state
\sigma_{23}
, and returning to external state
\sigma_{25}
.

(5)

{(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{3}});\sigma_{25}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma_{28}}
.
Purely external execution from
\sigma_{25}
to
\sigma_{28}
, scoped by
{\sigma_{3}}
.

Definition G.27.

For any module
M
where
M
is the internal module, external modules
\overline{M}
, and states
\sigma_{sc}
,
\sigma
,
\sigma_{1}
, …
\sigma_{n}
, and
\sigma^{\prime}
, we define:

(1)

{(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma^{\prime}}

\triangleq

\begin{cases}M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}\ \wedge\ \\
[\ \ \ \sigma=\sigma^{\prime}\,\wedge\,|\sigma_{sc}|\leq|\sigma|\,\wedge\,|\sigma_{sc}|\leq|\sigma^{\prime\prime}|\ \ \ \ \ \vee\\
\ \ \ \exists\sigma^{\prime\prime}[\,(\overline{M}\cdot M,\sigma_{sc});\,{\sigma}\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime\prime}\ \wedge\ {(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma^{\prime\prime}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma^{\prime}}\,]\ \ \ ]\end{cases}

(2)

{(\overline{M}\cdot M);\sigma{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{p}\,\sigma^{\prime}{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{1}\,}}

\triangleq

\begin{cases}M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}\ \wedge\\
\exists\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\ [\ \ (\overline{M}\cdot M,\sigma);\,{\sigma}\rightsquigarrow\sigma_{1}\,\wedge\,M,\sigma_{1}\models\mbox{{\texttt{pub}}}\ \wedge\\
\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma_{1}}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\ \wedge\ \overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\ \ ]\end{cases}

(3)

(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}\,{\textbf{pb}}\,{\epsilon}

\triangleq

(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma^{\prime\prime}

(4)

(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}\,{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{1}}

\triangleq

\exists\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime}.\begin{cases}\ \ {(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ {(\overline{M}\cdot M);\sigma_{1}^{\prime}{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{p}\,\sigma_{2}^{\prime}{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{1}\,}}\ \ \wedge\\
\ \ {(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma_{2}^{\prime}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma^{\prime}}\\
\end{cases}

(5)

(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}\,{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{1}...\sigma_{n}}

\triangleq

\exists\sigma_{1}^{\prime}.[\ \ (\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\,{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{1}}\ \wedge\ {(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma_{1}^{\prime}{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}\,{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{2}...\sigma_{n}}}\ \ ]

(6)

\overline{M}\cdot M;\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}

\triangleq

\exists n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}.\exists\sigma_{1},...\sigma_{n}.\ (\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma});\sigma{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}\,{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{1}...\sigma_{n}}

Note that

{(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma^{\prime}}
implies that
\sigma
is external, but does not
imply that
\sigma^{\prime}
is external.

{(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma});\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma^{\prime}}
.
On the other hand,
(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}\,{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{1}...\sigma_{n}}
implies that
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
are external, and
\sigma_{1}
, …
\sigma_{1}
are internal and public.
Finally, note that in part (
6
) above it is possible that
n=0
, and so

\overline{M}\cdot M;\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}
also holds when
Finally, note that the decomposition used in (
5
) is not unique, but since we only care for the public states this is of no importance.

Lemma
G.28
says that

(1)

Any terminating execution which starts at an external state (
\sigma
) consists of a number of external states interleaved with another number of terminating calls to public methods.

(2)

Any execution execution which starts at an external state (
\sigma
) and reaches another state (
\sigma^{\prime}
) also consists of a number of external states interleaved with another number of terminating calls to public methods, which may be followed by a call to some public method (at
\sigma_{2}
), and from where another execution, scoped by
\sigma_{2}
reaches
\sigma^{\prime}
.

Auxiliary Lemma G.28.

[Summarised Executions]
For module
M
, modules
\overline{M}
, and states
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
:

If
M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}
, then

(1)

M\cdot\overline{M};\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ \overline{M}\cdot M;\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}

(2)

M\cdot\overline{M};\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \ \ \ \ \Longrightarrow

(a)

\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \overline{M}\cdot M;\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \ \ \vee

(b)

\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \exists\sigma_{c},\sigma_{d}.[\ \overline{M}\cdot M;\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma_{c}\wedge\ \overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma_{c}}\rightsquigarrow\sigma_{d}\wedge\ M,\sigma_{c}\models\mbox{{\texttt{pub}}}\wedge\overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma_{d}}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime}\ ]

Auxiliary Lemma G.29.

[Preservation of Encapsulated Assertions]
For any module
M
, modules
\overline{M}
, assertion
A
, and
states
\sigma_{sc}
,
\sigma
,
\sigma_{1}
…
\sigma_{n}
,
\sigma_{a}
,
\sigma_{b}
and
\sigma^{\prime}
:

If

\hskip 14.22636ptM\vdash{Enc(A)}\ \wedge\ fv(A)=\emptyset\ \wedge\ M,\sigma,{k}\models{A}\ \wedge\ k\leq|\sigma_{sc}|
.

Then

(1)

M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}\ \wedge\ (\overline{M}\cdot M,\sigma_{sc});\,{\sigma}\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A}

(2)

(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A}

(3)

(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}}\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}\,{\textbf{pb}}\,{\sigma_{1}...\sigma_{n}}\ \ \wedge

\ \ \ \ \ \forall i\in[1..n].\forall\sigma_{f}.[\ \ M,\sigma_{i},{k}\models{A}\ \wedge\ M\cdot\overline{M};\,{\sigma_{i}}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma_{f}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ M,\sigma_{f},{k}\models{A}\ ]

\Longrightarrow

\ \ \ \ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A}

\ \ \ \ \ \wedge

\ \ \ \ \ \forall i\in[1..n].M,\sigma_{i},{k}\models{A}

\ \ \ \ \ \wedge

\ \ \ \ \ \forall i\in[1..n].\forall\sigma_{f}.[\ \ M\cdot\overline{M};\,{\sigma_{i}}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{\mathit{fin}}\,\sigma_{f}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ M,\sigma_{f},{k}\models{A}\ ]

Proof Sketch

(1)

is proven by Def. of
{Enc(\_)}
and the fact
|\sigma^{\prime}|\geq|\sigma_{sc}|
and therefore
k\leq|\sigma^{\prime}|
.
In particular, the step
(\overline{M}\cdot M,\sigma_{sc});\,{\sigma}\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}
may push or pop a frame onto
\sigma
.
If it pops a frame, then
M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A}
holds by definition.
If is pushes a frame, then
M,\sigma^{\prime}\models A
, by lemma
4.8
; this gives that
M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A}
.

(2)

by induction on the number of steps in
(\overline{M}\cdot M,\,{\sigma_{sc}});\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!_{e}\,\sigma^{\prime}
, and using (1).

(3)

by induction on the number of states appearing in
{\sigma_{1}...\sigma_{n}}
, and using (2).

End Proof Sketch

G.9.
Sequences, Sets, Substitutions and Free Variables

Our system makes heavy use of textual substitution, textual inequality, and the concept of free variables in assertions.

In this subsection we introduce some notation and some lemmas to deal with these concepts.
These concepts and lemmas are by no means novel; we list them here so as to use them more easily in the subsequent proofs.

Definition G.30 (Sequences, Disjointness, and Disjoint Concatenation).

For any variables
v
,
w
, and sequences of variables
\overline{v}
,
\overline{w}
we define:

•

v\in\overline{w}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \exists\overline{w_{1}},\overline{w_{1}}[\ {\overline{w}}=\overline{w_{1}},v,\overline{w_{2}}\ ]

•

v\#w\ \ \triangleq\ \ \neg(v\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}w)
.

•

\overline{v}\subseteq\overline{w}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \forall v.[\ v\in\overline{v}\ \Rightarrow\ v\in\overline{w}\ ]

•

\overline{v}\#\overline{w}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \forall v\in\overline{v}.\forall w\in\overline{w}.[\ v\#w\ ]

•

\overline{v}\cap\overline{w}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \overline{u},\ \ \ \ \mbox{such that}\ \forall u.[\ \ u\in\overline{v}\cap\overline{w}\ \ \Leftrightarrow\ \ [\ u\in\overline{v}\ \wedge\ u\in\overline{w}\ ]

•

\overline{v}\setminus\overline{w}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \overline{u},\ \ \ \ \mbox{such that}\ \forall u.[\ \ u\in\overline{v}\setminus\overline{w}\ \ \Leftrightarrow\ \ [\ u\in\overline{v}\ \wedge\ u\notin\overline{w}\ ]

•

\overline{v};\overline{w}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \overline{v}
,
\overline{w}
if
\overline{v}\#\overline{w}
and undefined otherwise.

Lemma G.31 (Substitutions and Free Variables).

For any sequences of variables
\overline{x}
,
\overline{y}
,
\overline{z}
,
\overline{v}
,
\overline{w}
, a variable
w
, any assertion
A
, we have

(1)

\overline{x}[\overline{y/x}]=\overline{y}

(2)

\overline{x}\#\overline{y}\ \ \Rightarrow\ \ \overline{y}[\overline{z/x}]=\overline{y}

(3)

\overline{z}\subseteq\overline{y}\ \ \Rightarrow\ \ \overline{y}[\overline{z/x}]\subseteq\overline{y}

(4)

\overline{y}\subseteq\overline{z}\ \ \Rightarrow\ \ \overline{y}[\overline{z/x}]\subseteq\overline{z}

(5)

\overline{x}\#\overline{y}\ \ \Rightarrow\ \ {\overline{z}}[\overline{y/x}]\#\overline{x}

(6)

{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A[\overline{y/x}])\,=\,{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A)[\overline{y/x}]

(7)

{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A)\,=\,\overline{x};\overline{v},\ \ \ {\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A[\overline{y/x}])\,=\,\overline{y};\overline{w}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ \overline{v}\,=\,(\overline{y}\cap\overline{v});\overline{w}

(8)

\overline{v}\#\overline{x}\#\overline{y}\#\overline{u}\ \ \ \ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ w[\overline{u/x}][\overline{v/y}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}w[\overline{v/y}][\overline{u/x}]

(9)

\overline{v}\#\overline{x}\#\overline{y}\#\overline{u}\ \ \ \ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ A[\overline{u/x}][\overline{v/y}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}A[\overline{v/y}][\overline{u/x}]

(10)

(fv(A[\overline{y/x}])\setminus\overline{y})\,\#\,\overline{x}

(11)

Proof.

(1)

by induction on the number of elements in
\overline{x}

(2)

by induction on the number of elements in
\overline{y}

(3)

by induction on the number of elements in
\overline{y}

(4)

by induction on the number of elements in
\overline{y}

(5)

by induction on the structure of
A

(6)

by induction on the structure of
A

(7)

Assume that

(ass1)\ \ \ {\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A)\,=\,\overline{x};\overline{v},

(ass2)\ \ \ {\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A[\overline{y/x}])\,=\,\overline{y};\overline{w}

We define:

(a)\ \ \ \overline{y_{0}}\triangleq\overline{v}\cap\overline{y},\ \ \ \overline{v_{2}}\triangleq\overline{v}\setminus\overline{y},\ \ \ \overline{y_{1}}=\overline{y_{0}}[\overline{x/y}]

This gives:

(b)\ \ \ \overline{y_{0}}\#\overline{v_{2}}

(c)\ \ \ \overline{v}=\overline{y_{0}};\overline{v_{2}}

(d)\ \ \ \overline{y_{1}}\subseteq\overline{y}

(e)\ \ \ \overline{v_{2}}[\overline{y/x}]=\overline{v_{2}}
, from assumption and (a) we have
\overline{x}\#\overline{v}_{2}
and by Lemma
G.31
) part (
2
)

We now calculate

\begin{array}[]{lcll}\ \ \ {\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A[\overline{y/x}])&=&(\overline{x};\overline{v})[\overline{y/x}]&\mbox{ by (ass1), and Lemma \ref{l:sfs} part (\ref{l:sfs:three}).}\\
&=&(\overline{x};\overline{y_{0}};\overline{v_{2}})[\overline{y/x}]&\mbox{ by (c) above }\\
&=&\overline{x}[\overline{y/x}],\,\overline{y_{0}}[\overline{y/x}],\overline{v_{2}}[\overline{y/x}]&\mbox{ by distributivity of $[../..]$ }\\
&=&\overline{y},\,\overline{y_{1}},\overline{v_{2}}&\mbox{ by Lemma \ref{l:sfs} part (\ref{l:sfs:zero}), (a), and (e). }\\
&=&\overline{y};\overline{v_{2}}&\mbox{ because (d), and $\overline{y}\#\overline{v_{2}}$ }\\
\ \ \ {\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A[\overline{y/x}])&=&\overline{y};\overline{w}&\mbox{ by (ass2)}\end{array}

The above gives that
\overline{v_{2}}=\overline{w}
. This, together with (a) and (c) give that
\overline{v}=(\overline{y}\cap\overline{v});\overline{w}

(8)

By case analysis on whether
w\in\overline{x}
… etc

(9)

By induction on the structure of
A
, and the guarantee from (
8
).

(10)

We take a variable sequence
\overline{z}
such that

(a)\ \ {\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A)\subseteq\overline{x};\overline{z}

This gives that

(b)\ \ \overline{x}\#\overline{z}

Part (
6
) of our lemma and (a) give

(c)\ \ {\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A[\overline{y/x}])\subseteq\overline{y},\overline{z}

Therefore

(d)\ \ {\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A[\overline{y/x}])\setminus{\overline{y}}\subseteq\overline{z}

The above, together with (b) conclude the proof

∎

Lemma G.32 (Substitutions and Adaptations).

For any sequences of variables
\overline{x}
,
\overline{y}
, sequences of expressions
\overline{e}
, and any assertion
A
, we have

•

\overline{x}\#\overline{y}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ {(A[\overline{e/x}])}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\overline{y}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}({A}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\overline{y})[\overline{e/x}]

Proof.

We first consider
A
to be
\langle\!\!\langle e\rangle\!\!\rangle_{0}
, and just take one variable. Then,

\ \ \ \ {{(\langle\!\!\langle e_{0}\rangle\!\!\rangle[e/x])}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\overline{y}}\ \mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}\ {\langle\!\!\langle e_{0}[{e/x}]\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!y}
,

and

\ \ \ \ ({\langle\!\!\langle e_{0}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\overline{y})[e/x]\ \mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}\ \langle\!\!\langle e_{0}[{e/x}]\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!y[{e/x}]
.

When
x\#y
then the two assertions from above are textually equal.
The rest follows by induction on the length of
\overline{x}
and the structure of
A
.
∎

Lemma G.33.

For assertion
A
, variables
\overline{x}
,
\overline{v}
,
\overline{y}
,
\overline{w}
,
\overline{v_{1}}
,
addresses
\overline{\alpha_{x}}
,
\overline{\alpha_{y}}
,
\overline{\alpha_{v}}
, and
\overline{\alpha_{v_{1}}}

If

(1)

{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A)\,\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}\,\overline{x};\overline{v}
,
{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A[\overline{y/x}])\,\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}\,\overline{y};\overline{w}
,

(2)

\forall x\in{\overline{x}}.[\ x[\overline{y/x}][\overline{\alpha_{y}/y}]\ =\ x[\overline{\alpha_{x}/x}]\ ]

(3)

\overline{v}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}\overline{v_{1}};\overline{w}
,
\overline{v_{1}}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}\overline{y}\cap\overline{v}
,
\overline{\alpha_{v,1}}=\overline{v_{1}}[\overline{\alpha_{v}/v}]

then

•

A[\overline{y/x}][\overline{\alpha_{y}/y}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}A[\overline{\alpha_{x}/x}][\overline{\alpha_{v,1}/v_{1}}]

Proof.

From Lemma
G.31
, part
7
, we obtain
(*)\ \ \overline{v}\,=\,(\overline{y}\cap\overline{v});\overline{w}

We first prove that

\hskip 85.35826pt\ \ (**)\ \ \forall z\in{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A)[\ z[\overline{y/x}][\overline{\alpha_{y}/y}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}z[\overline{\alpha_{x}/x}][\overline{\alpha_{v,1}/v_{1}}]
.

Take any arbitrary
z\in{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A)
.

Then, by assumptions
1
and
3
, and (*) we have that either
z\in\overline{x}
, or
z\in\overline{v_{1}}
, or

z\in\overline{w}
.

1st Case:

z\in\overline{x}
.
Then, there exists some
y_{z}\in\overline{y}
, and some
\alpha_{z}\in\overline{\alpha_{y}}
, such that
z[\overline{y/x}]=y_{z}
and

y_{z}[\overline{\alpha_{y}/y}]=\alpha_{z}
.
On the other hand, by part
2
we obtain, that
z[\overline{\alpha_{x}/x}]=\alpha_{z}
.
And because
\overline{v_{1}}\#\overline{\alpha x}
we also have that
\alpha_{z}[\overline{\alpha_{v,1}/v_{1}}]
=
\alpha_{z}
.
This concludes the case.

2nd Case:

z\in\overline{v_{1}}
, which means that
z\in\overline{y}\cap\overline{v}
. Then, because
\overline{x}\#\overline{v}
, we have that
z[\overline{y/x}]=z
.
And because
z\in\overline{y}
, we obtain that there exists a
\alpha_{z}
, so that
z[\overline{\alpha_{y}/y}]=\alpha_{z}
.
Similarly, because
\overline{x}\#\overline{v}
, we also obtain that
z[\overline{\alpha_{x}/x}]=z
.
And because
\overline{v_{1}}\subseteq\overline{y}
, we also obtain that
z[\overline{\alpha_{v,1}/v_{1}}]
=
z[\overline{\alpha_{y}/y}]
.
This concludes the case.

3rd Case:

z\in\overline{w}
. From part
1
of the assumptions and from (*) we obtain
\overline{w}\#\overline{y}\#\overline{x}
, which implies that
z[\overline{y/x}][\overline{\alpha_{y}/y}]
=
z
.
Moreover, (*) also gives that
\overline{w}\#\overline{v_{1}}
, and this gives that

z[\overline{\alpha_{x}/x}][\overline{\alpha_{v,1}/v_{1}}]
=
z
.
This concludes the case

The lemma follows from (*) and structural induction on
A
.
∎

G.10.
Reachability, Heap Identity, and their properties

We consider states with the same heaps (
\sigma\sim\sigma^{\prime}
) and properties about reachability of an address from another address (
Reach(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})_{\sigma}
).

Definition G.34.

For any state
\sigma
, addresses
\alpha
,
\alpha^{\prime}
, we define

•

Reach(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})_{\sigma}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \exists\overline{f}.[\ \lfloor\alpha.\overline{f}\rfloor_{\sigma}=\alpha^{\prime}\ ]

•

\sigma\sim\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \exists\chi,\overline{\phi_{1}},\overline{\phi_{2}}.[\ \sigma=(\overline{\phi_{1}},\chi)\ \wedge\ \sigma^{\prime}=(\overline{\phi_{1}},\chi)\ ]

Lemma G.35.

For any module
M
, state
\sigma
, addresses
\alpha
,
\alpha^{\prime}
,
\alpha^{\prime\prime}

(1)

M,\sigma\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha^{\prime}}\ \wedge\ Reach(\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime})_{\sigma}\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ M,\sigma\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha^{\prime\prime}}

(2)

\sigma\sim\sigma^{\prime}\ \Longrightarrow\ \ [\ Reach(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})_{\sigma}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ Reach(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})_{\sigma^{\prime}}\ ]

(3)

\sigma\sim\sigma^{\prime}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ [\ M,\sigma\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ M,\sigma^{\prime}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\ ]

(4)

\sigma\sim\sigma^{\prime}\ \wedge\ {\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A)=\emptyset\wedge\ {Stbl(A)}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ [\ M,\sigma\models A\ \Longleftrightarrow\ M,\sigma^{\prime}\models A\ ]

Proof.

(1)

By unfolding/folding the definitions

(2)

By unfolding/folding the definitions

(3)

By unfolding/folding definitions.

(4)

By structural induction on
A
, and Lemma
G.35
part
3
.

∎

G.11.
Preservation of assertions when pushing or popping frames

In this section we discuss the preservation of satisfaction of assertions when calling methods or returning from methods –
i.e.
when pushing or popping frames.
Namely, since pushing/popping frames does not modify the heap, these operations should preserve satisfaction of some assertion
A
, up to the fact that a) passing an object as a parameter of a a result might break its protection, and
b) the bindings of variables change with pushing/popping frames.
To deal with a) upon method call,
we require that the fame being pushed or the frame to which we return is internal (
M,\sigma^{\prime}\models\mbox{{\texttt{intl}}}
), or require the adapted version of an assertion (
i.e.

{{A}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\overline{v}}
rather than
A
).
To deal with b) we either require that there are no free variables in
A
, or we break the free variables of
A
into two parts,
i.e.

{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A_{in})=\overline{v_{1}};\overline{v_{2}}
, where the value of
\overline{v_{3}}
in the caller is the same as that of
\overline{v_{1}}
in the called frame.
This is described in lemmas
G.40
-
G.42
.

We have four lemmas: Lemma
G.40
describes preservation from a caller to an internal called, lemma
G.41

describes preservation from a caller to any called, Lemma
G.42
describes preservation from an internal called to a caller, and Lemma
G.43
describes preservation from an any called to a caller,
These four lemmas are used in the soundness proof for the four Hoare rules about method calls, as given in Fig.
8
.

In the rest of this section we will first introduce some further auxiliary concepts and lemmas,
and then state, discuss and prove Lemmas
G.40
-
G.42
.

Plans for next three subsections

Lemmas
G.40
-
G.41
are quite complex, because they deal with substitution of some of the assertions’ free variables.
We therefore approach the proofs gradually:
We first state and prove a very simplified version of Lemmas
G.40
-
G.41
, where the assertion (
A_{in}
or
A_{out}
) is only about protection and only contains addresses; this is the only basic assertion which is not
Stbl
.
We then state a slightly more general version of Lemmas
G.40
-
G.41
, where the assertion (
A_{in}
or
A_{out}
) is variable-free.

G.12.
Preservation of variable-free simple protection when pushing/popping frames

We now move to consider preservation of variable-free assertions about protection when pushing/popping frames

Lemma G.36 (From caller to called - protected, and variable-free).

For any address
\alpha
, addresses
\overline{\alpha}
, states
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
,
and frame
\phi
.

If
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\,{\triangledown}\,\phi

then

(1)

M,\sigma,{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}\ \ \wedge\ \ M,\sigma^{\prime}\models\mbox{{\texttt{intl}}}\ \ \wedge\ \ Rng(\phi)\subseteq LocRchbl(\sigma)\hfill\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}

(2)

M,\sigma,{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\overline{\alpha}}\ \ \wedge\ \ Rng(\phi)\subseteq{\overline{\alpha}}\hfill\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ M,{\sigma^{\prime}}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}

(3)

M,\sigma,{k}\models{{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}\wedge{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\overline{\alpha}}}\ \ \wedge\ \ Rng(\phi)\subseteq{\overline{\alpha}}\hfill\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}

Proof.

(1)

(1) Take any
\alpha^{\prime}\in LocRchbl(\sigma^{\prime})
.
Then, by assumptions, we have
\alpha^{\prime}\in LocRchbl(\sigma)
.
This gives, again by assumptions, that
M,\sigma\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha^{\prime}}
.
By the construction of
\sigma
, and lemma
G.35
part
1
, we obtain that (2)
M,\sigma^{\prime}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha^{\prime}}
.
From (1) and (2) and because
M,\sigma^{\prime}\models\mbox{{\texttt{intl}}}
we obtain that
M,\sigma^{\prime}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}
.
Then apply lemma
G.35
part
G.5
, and we are done.

(2)

By unfolding and folding the definitions, and application of Lemma
G.35
part
1
.

(3)

By part
G.35
part
2
and
G.5
.

Notice that part
G.36
requires that the called (
\sigma^{\prime}
) is internal, but parts
2
and
3
do not.

Notice also that the conclusion in part
2
is
M,{\sigma^{\prime}}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}
and not
M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}
.
This is so, because it is possible that
M,\sigma\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\overline{\alpha}}
but
M,\sigma\not\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}
.

∎

Lemma G.37 (From called to caller – protected, and variable-free).

For any states
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
, variable
v
, address
\alpha_{v}
,
addresses
\overline{\alpha}
,
and statement
stmt
.

If
\ \sigma^{\prime}=(\sigma\,{\vartriangle}\,)[v\!\mapsto\alpha_{v}][\mbox{{\texttt{cont}}}\!\mapsto\!stmt]
,

then

(1)

M,\sigma,{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}\ \ \wedge\ \ k<|\sigma|\ \ \wedge\ \ M,\sigma\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha_{v}}\hfill\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}
.

(2)

M,\sigma\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}\ \ \wedge\ \ \overline{\alpha}\subseteq LocRchbl(\sigma)\hfill\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\overline{\alpha}}
.

Proof.

(1)

(1) Take any
i\!\in\![k..|\sigma^{\prime}|)
.
Then, by definitions and assumption, we have
M,{\sigma[i]}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}
.
Take any
\alpha^{\prime}\!\in\!LocRchbl(\sigma[i])
.
We obtain that
M,{\sigma[i]}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha^{\prime}}
.
Therefore,
M,{\sigma[i]}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}
.
Moreover,
{\sigma[i]}
=
{\sigma^{\prime}[i]}
, and we therefore obtain (2)
M,{\sigma^{\prime}[i]}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}
.

(3) Now take a
\alpha^{\prime}\!\in\!LocRchbl(\sigma^{\prime})
.

Then, we have that either (A):
\alpha^{\prime}\!\in\!LocRchbl(\sigma[|\sigma^{\prime}|])
, or (B):
Reach(\alpha_{r},\alpha^{\prime})_{\sigma^{\prime}}
.

In the case of (A): Because
k,|\sigma|=|\sigma^{\prime}|+1
, and because
M,\sigma,{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}

we have
M,\sigma\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha^{\prime}}
.
Because
\sigma\sim\sigma^{\prime}
and Lemma
G.35
part
3
, we obtain (A’)
M,\sigma^{\prime}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha^{\prime}}

In the case of (B): Because
\sigma\sim\sigma^{\prime}
and lemma
G.35
part
2
, we obtain
Reach(\alpha_{r},\alpha^{\prime})_{\sigma}
.
Then, applying Lemma
G.35
part
3
and assumptions, we obtain (B’)
M,\sigma^{\prime}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\alpha^{\prime}}
.

From (3), (A), (A’), (B) and (B’) we obtain: (4)
M,\sigma^{\prime}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle}
.

With (1), (2), (4) and Lemma
G.35
part
4
we are done.

(2)

From the definitions we obtain that
M,\sigma\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\overline{\alpha}}
.
Because
\sigma\sim\sigma^{\prime}
and lemma
G.35
part
3
, we obtain
M,\sigma^{\prime}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\overline{\alpha}}
.
And because of lemma
G.4
, part
3
, we obtain
M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{\langle\!\!\langle\alpha\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\overline{\alpha}}
.

∎

G.14.
Preservation of assertions when pushing or popping frames – stated and proven

Lemma G.40 (From caller to internal called).

For any assertion
A_{in}
, states
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
,

\overline{v_{1}}
,
\overline{v_{2}}
,
\overline{v_{3}}
,
\overline{v_{4}}
,
\overline{v_{6}}
, and frame
\phi
.

If

(1)

{Stb^{+}(A_{in})}
,

(2)

{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A_{in})=\overline{v_{1}};\overline{v_{2}}
17
17

17

As we said earlier. this gives also that the variable sequences are pairwise disjoint,
i.e.

\overline{v_{1}}\#\overline{v_{2}}
.
,

{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A_{in}[\overline{v_{3}/v_{1}}])=\overline{v_{3}};\overline{v_{4}}
,
\overline{v_{6}}\triangleq\overline{v_{2}}\cap\overline{v_{3}};\overline{v_{4}}
,

(3)

\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\,{\triangledown}\,\phi\ \ \ \ \wedge\ \ \ Rng(\phi)=\overline{\lfloor v_{3}\rfloor_{\sigma}}

(4)

\overline{\lfloor v_{1}\rfloor_{\sigma^{\prime}}}=\overline{\lfloor v_{3}\rfloor_{\sigma}}
,

then

(1)

(2)

.

Discussion of Lemma

In lemma
G.40
, state
\sigma
is the state right before pushing the new frame on the stack, while
state
\sigma^{\prime}
is the state right after pushing the frame on the stack.
That is,
\sigma
is the last state before entering the method body, and
\sigma^{\prime}
is the first state after entering the method body.

A_{in}
stands for the method’s precondition, while the variables
\overline{v_{1}}
stand for the formal parameters of the method,
and
\overline{v_{3}}
stand for the actual parameters of the call.
Therefore,
\overline{v_{1}}
is the domain of the new frame, and
\overline{\sigma}{v_{3}}
is its range.
The variables
\overline{v_{6}}
are the free variables of
A_{in}
which are not in
\overline{v_{1}}
–
c.f.
Lemma
G.31
part
(
7
).
Therefore if (
1
) the callee is internal, and

A_{in}[\overline{v_{3}/v_{1}}]
holds at the call point, or
if (
2
)
{(A_{in}[\overline{v_{3}/v_{1}}])\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(\overline{v_{3}})}

holds at the call point,
then
A_{in}[\overline{.../v_{6}1}]
holds right after pushing
\phi
onto the stack.
Notice the difference in the conclusion in (
1
) and (
2
): in the first case we have deep satisfaction, while in the second case we only have shallow satisfaction.

Proof.

We will use
\overline{\alpha_{1}}
as short for
\{\lfloor v_{1}\rfloor_{\sigma^{\prime}}
, and
\overline{\alpha_{3}}
as short for
\overline{\lfloor v_{3}\rfloor_{\sigma}}
.

We aslo define
\overline{v_{6,1}}\triangleq\overline{v_{2}}\cap\overline{v_{3}}
,
\overline{\alpha_{6,1}}\triangleq\overline{v_{6,1}}[\overline{\lfloor v_{6}\rfloor_{\sigma}/v_{6}}]

We establish that

\ \ \ (*)\ \ \ A_{in}[\overline{v_{3}/v_{1}}][\overline{{\lfloor v_{3}\rfloor_{\sigma}}/v_{3}}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}A_{in}[\overline{\alpha_{1}/v_{1}}][\overline{\alpha_{6,1}/v_{6,1}}]

This holds by By Lemma
G.33
and assumption
4
of the current lemma.

And we define

(1)

Assume

The above, and Lemma

4.5
the above gives

By Lemma

, we obtain

M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A_{in}[\overline{\lfloor v_{6}\rfloor_{\sigma}/v_{6}}]}

(2)

.

this implies that

M,\sigma,{k}\models{((A_{in}[\overline{v_{3}/v_{1}}])\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(\overline{v_{3}}))[\overline{{\alpha_{3}}/v_{3}}]}

By (*) from above we have

Lemma G.41 (From caller to any called).

For any assertion
A_{in}
, states
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}
,
variables
\overline{v_{3}}

statement
stmt
, and frame
\phi
.

If

(1)

{Stb^{+}(A_{in})}
,

(2)

{\hskip-1.0ptF\hskip-1.0pt}v(A_{in})=\emptyset
,

(3)

\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\,{\triangledown}\,\phi\ \ \ \ \wedge\ \ \ Rng(\phi)=\overline{\lfloor v_{3}\rfloor_{\sigma}},

then

(1)

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A_{in}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(\overline{v_{3}})}\hfill\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ M,\sigma^{\prime}\models{A_{in}}
.

(2)

M,\sigma,{k}\models{(A_{in}\wedge({A_{in}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(\overline{v_{3}})}))}\hfill\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \ M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A_{in}}

Proof.

(1)

Assume that

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A_{in}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(\overline{v_{3}})}

By Lemma
4.5
this implies that

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A_{in}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(\lfloor v_{3}\rfloor_{\sigma})}
.

We now have a variable-free assertion, and by Lemma

, part
2
, we obtain

M,\sigma^{\prime}\models A_{in}
.

(2)

Assume that

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A_{in}\wedge A_{in}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(\overline{v_{3}})}

By Lemma

this implies that

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A_{in}\wedge A_{in}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(\lfloor v_{3}\rfloor_{\sigma})}
.

We now have a variable-free assertion, and by Lemma

, part

, we obtain

M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{\models}A_{in}
.

Discussion of Lemma
G.41

In this lemma, as in lemma
G.40
,

\sigma
stands for the last state before entering the method body, and
\sigma^{\prime}
for the first state after entering the method body.

A_{in}
stands for a module invariant in which all free variables have been substituted by addresses.
The lemma is intended for external calls, and therefore we have no knowledge of the method’s formal parameters.
The variables
\overline{v_{3}}
stand for the actual parameters of the call, and therefore

\overline{\lfloor v_{3}\rfloor_{\sigma}}
is the range of the new frame.
Therefore if (
1
) the adapted version,

{A_{in}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(\overline{v_{3}})}
, holds at the call point,
then the unadapted version,
A_{in}
holds right after pushing
\phi
onto the stack.
Notice that even though the premise of (
1
) requires deep satisfaction, the conclusion promises
only weak satisfaction.
Moreover, if (
2
) the adapted as well as the unadapted version,

A_{in}\wedge{A_{in}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(\overline{v_{3}})}

holds at the call point,
then the unadapted version,
A_{in}
holds right after pushing
\phi
onto the stack.
Notice the difference in the conclusion in (
1
) and (
2
): in the first case we have shallow satisfaction, while in the second case we only have deep satisfaction.

Lemma G.42 (From internal called to caller).

Discussion of Lemma
G.42

State
\sigma
stands for the last state in the method body, and
\sigma^{\prime}
for the first state after exiting the method call.

A_{out}
stands for a method postcondition.
The lemma is intended for internal calls, and therefore we know the method’s formal parameters.
The variables
\overline{v_{1}}
stand for the formal parameters of the method, and
\overline{v_{3}}
stand for the actual parameters of the call.
Therefore the formal parameters of the called have the same values as the actual parameters in the caller

\overline{\lfloor v_{1}\rfloor_{\sigma}}\,=\,\overline{\lfloor v_{3}\rfloor_{\sigma^{\prime}}}
.
Therefore (
1
) and (
2
)
promise that if the postcondition
A_{out}
holds before popping the frame, then it also holds after popping frame after replacing the
the formal parameters by the actual parameters
A_{out}[\overline{v_{3}/v_{1}}]
.
As in earlier lemmas, there is an important difference between (
1
) and (
2
):
In (
1
), we require
deep satisfaction at the called
,
and obtain at the deep satisfaction of the
unadapted
version (
A_{out}[\overline{v_{3}/v_{1}}]
) at the return point;
while in (
2
), we only require
shallow satisfaction at the called
,
and obtain deep satisfaction of the
adapted
version (
{(A_{out}[\overline{v_{3}/v_{1}}])\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\overline{v_{5}}}
),
at the return point.

Proof.

We use the following short hands:
\alpha
as
\overline{\lfloor res\rfloor_{\sigma}}
,

\overline{\alpha_{1}}
for
\overline{\lfloor v_{1}\rfloor_{\sigma}}
,
\overline{\alpha_{5}}
as short for
\overline{\lfloor v_{5}\rfloor_{\sigma^{\prime}}}
.

(1)

Assume that

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A_{out}\wedge{A_{out}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}res}}

By Lemma
4.5
this implies that

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A_{out}[\overline{\alpha_{1}/v_{1}}]\wedge{(A_{out}[\overline{\alpha_{1}/v_{1}}])\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\alpha}}
.

We now have a variable-free assertion,
and by Lemma
G.39
, part
1
, we obtain

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A_{out}[\overline{\alpha_{1}/v_{1}}]}
.

By Lemma
4.5
, and because
\overline{\lfloor v_{1}\rfloor_{\sigma}}\,=\,\overline{\lfloor v_{3}\rfloor_{\sigma^{\prime}}}
this implies that

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A_{out}[\overline{v_{3}/v_{1}}]}
.

(2)

Assume that

M,\sigma\models A_{out}

By Lemma
4.5
this implies that

M,\sigma\models A_{out}[\overline{\alpha_{1}/v_{1}}]

We now have a variable-free assertion,
and by Lemma
G.39
, part
2
, we obtain

M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A_{out}[\overline{\alpha_{1}/v_{1}}]\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\overline{\alpha}_{5}}

By Lemma

, and because

and
\alpha_{5}=\overline{\lfloor v_{5}\rfloor_{\sigma^{\prime}}}

∎

Lemma G.43 (From any called to caller).

Discussion of lemma
G.43

, Similarly to lemma
G.42
,
in this lemma, state
\sigma
stands for the last state in the method body, and
\sigma^{\prime}
for the first state after exiting the method call.

A_{out}
stands for a method postcondition.
The lemma is meant to apply to external calls, and therefore, we do not know the method’s formal parameters,

A_{out}
is meant to stand for a module invariant where all the free variables have been substituted by addresses –

i.e.

A_{out}
has no free variables.
The variables
\overline{v_{3}}
stand for the actual parameters of the call.
Parts (
1
) and (
2
)
promise that if the postcondition
A_{out}
holds before popping the frame, then it its adapted version
also holds after popping frame (
{A_{out}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\overline{v_{5}}}
).
As in earlier lemmas, there is an important difference between (
1
) and (
2
)
In (
1
), we require
shallow satisfaction at the called
,
and obtain deep satisfaction of the
adapted
version (
{A_{out}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\overline{v_{5}}}
) at the return point;
while in (
2
), we require
deep satisfaction at the called
,
and obtain deep satisfaction of the
conjuction
of the
unadapted
with the
adapted
version (
{A_{out}\,\wedge\,{A_{out}\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\overline{v_{5}}}}
),
at the return point.

G.15.

-
G.41

As we said earlier, Lemmas

-
G.41

. we will use Lemma

) and Lemma

part (
1
).
In the proof of soundness of

part (

) and Lemma
G.42
part (
2
).
In the proof of soundness of

we will use Lemma
G.41
part (
1
) and Lemma
G.43
part (
1
).
And finally, in the proof of soundness of

we will use Lemma
G.41
part (
2
) and Lemma
G.43
part (
2
).

G.17.
Proof Sketch of Theorem
7.3
– part (B)

Proof Sketch

By induction on the cases for the specification
S
. If it is a method spec, then the theorem follows from
7.3
. If it is a conjunction, then by inductive hypothesis.

The interesting case is
S\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny txt}}}}{{=}}}{\ \rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{{$\mathbb{A}\!\!$}}{\overline{x:C}}.{{\{}}{A}{{\}}}}
.

Assume that

M,\sigma,{k}\models{A}[\overline{\alpha/x}]
, that
M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}
,
that
M\cdot\overline{M};\,{\sigma}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime}
, and that
M,\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}
,

We want to show that
M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A}[\overline{\alpha/x}]
.

Then, by lemma

G.28
, we obtain that either

(1)
\overline{M}\cdot M;\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma^{\prime}
, or

(2)
\exists\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}.[\ \overline{M}\cdot M;\sigma{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\!\!\!\!_{e,p}\,\sigma_{1}\wedge\ \overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma_{1}}\rightsquigarrow\sigma_{2}\wedge\ M,\sigma_{2}\models\mbox{{\texttt{pub}}}\wedge\overline{M}\cdot M;\,{\sigma_{2}}{\rightsquigarrow^{*}}\sigma^{\prime}\ ]

In Case (1), we apply
G.29
, part (3). In order to fulfill the second premise of Lemma
G.29
, part (3), we make use of the fact that
\vdash M
, apply the rule
Method
, and theorem
7.3
.
This gives us
M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A}[\overline{\alpha/x}]

In Case (2), we proceed as in (1) and obtain that
M,\sigma_{1},{k}\models{A}[\overline{\alpha/x}]
. Because
M\vdash{Enc(A)}
, we also obtain that

M,\sigma_{2},{k}\models{A}[\overline{\alpha/x}]
.
Since we are now executing a public method, and because
\vdash M
, we can apply
Invariant
, and theorem
7.3
, and obtain
M,\sigma^{\prime},{k}\models{A}[\overline{\alpha/x}]

End Proof Sketch

Appendix H
Proving Limited Effects for the Shop/Account Example

In Section
2
we introduced a
Shop
that allows clients to make purchases through the

buy
method.
The body if this method includes a method call to an unknown external object (
buyer.pay(...)
).

In this section we use our Hoare logic from Section
6
to outline the proof that the
buy
method
does not expose the
Shop
’s
Account
, its
Key
, or allow the
Account
’s balance to be illicitly modified.

We outline the proof that
M_{good}\vdash S_{2}
, and that
M_{fine}\vdash S_{2}
.
We also show why
M_{bad}\not\vdash S_{2}
.

We rewrite the code of
M_{good}
and so
M_{fine}

so that it adheres to the syntax as defined in Fig.
4
(§
H.1
).
We extend the specification
S_{2}
, so that is also makes a specification for the private method
set
(§
H.2
).
After that, we outline the proofs that
M_{good}\vdash S_{2}
, and that
M_{fine}\vdash S_{2}
(in §
H.2
),
and that
M_{good}\vdash S_{3}
, and that
M_{fine}\vdash S_{3}
(§
H.4
).
These proofs have been mechanized in Coq, and the source code will be
submitted as an artefact. We also discuss why
M_{bad}\not\vdash S_{2}
(§
H.3.2
).

H.2.
Proving that
M_{good}
and
M_{fine}
satisfy
S_{2}

We redefine
S_{2}
so that it also describes the behaviour of method
send
. and have:

{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ S_{2a}}

\triangleq

\{\,\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}\wedge\mbox{{\texttt{e}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{external}}}\wedge\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{e}}}\,\}\,

\ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{{\texttt{private Shop}}}::\mbox{{\texttt{send}}}({\mbox{{\texttt{buyer}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{external}}},\mbox{{\texttt{anItem}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Item}}}})

\{\,\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!e\,\}\ {\,\parallel\,\ \{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!e\}}

{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ S_{2b}}

\triangleq

\{\,\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}\wedge\mbox{{\texttt{a.blnce}}}=\mbox{{\texttt{b}}}\,\}\,

\ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{{\texttt{private Shop}}}::\mbox{{\texttt{send}}}({\mbox{{\texttt{buyer}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{external}}},\mbox{{\texttt{anItem}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Item}}}})

\{\,\mbox{{\texttt{a.blnce}}}=\mbox{{\texttt{b}}}\,\}\ {\,\parallel\,\ \{\mbox{{\texttt{a.blnce}}}=\mbox{{\texttt{b}}}\}}

\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ S_{2,strong}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \ S_{2}\ \wedge\ S_{2a}\ \wedge\ S_{2b}

For brevity we only show that
buy
satisfies our scoped invariants, as the all other methods of
the
M
good
interface are relatively simple, and do not make any external calls.

To write our proofs more succinctly, we will use
ClassId
::
methId
.
body
as a shorthand for the method body of
methId
defined in
ClassId
.

Lemma H.1 (
M_{good}
satisfies
S_{2,strong}
).

M_{good}\vdash S_{2,strong}

Proof Outline

In order to prove that

M_{good}\vdash\ \rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{{$\mathbb{A}\!\!$}}{\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}}.{{\{}}{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}{{\}}}

we have to apply
Invariant
from Fig.
9
.
That is, for each class
C
defined in
M_{good}
, and for each public method
m
in
C
, with parameters
\overline{y:D}
, we have to prove that

\displaystyle M_{good}\ \vdash\ \

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{this}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{C}}},\,\overline{y:D},\,\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}\,\wedge\,{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\ \wedge\ \langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!(\mbox{{\texttt{this}}},\overline{y})\ \}

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073pt\mbox{{\texttt{C}}}::\mbox{{\texttt{m}}}.\mbox{{\texttt{body}}}\

\displaystyle\{\ {\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\ \wedge\ \ \langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{res}}}\ \}\ ||\ \{\ {\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\ \}

Thus, we need to prove three Hoare quadruples: one for

H.3.
Showing that
M_{bad}
does not satisfy
S_{2}
nor
S_{3}

H.3.1.
M_{bad}
does not satisfy
S_{2}

M_{bad}
does not satisfy
S_{2}
. We can argue this semantically (as in §
H.3.2
), and also in terms of the proof system (as in
H.3.3
).

H.3.2.
M_{bad}\nvDash S_{2}

The reason is that
M_{bad}
exports the public method
set
, which updates the key without any checks.
So, it could start in a state where the key of the account was protected, and then update it to something not protected.

In more detail: Take any state
\sigma
, where
M_{bad},\sigma\models a_{0}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}},k_{0}:\mbox{{\texttt{Key}}}\wedge\langle\!\!\langle a_{0}.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle
.
Assume also that
M_{bad},\sigma\models\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}
.
Finally, assume that the continuation in
\sigma
consists of
a_{0}.\mbox{{\texttt{set}}}(k_{0})
.
Then we obtain that
M_{bad},\sigma\leadsto^{*}\sigma^{\prime}
, where
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[a_{0}.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\mapsto k_{0}]
.
We also have that
M_{bad},\sigma^{\prime}\models\mbox{{\texttt{extl}}}
, and because
k_{0}
is a local variable, we also have that
M_{bad},\sigma^{\prime}\nvDash\langle\!\!\langle k_{0}\rangle\!\!\rangle
.
Moreover,
M_{bad},\sigma^{\prime}\models a_{0}.\mbox{{\texttt{krey}}}=k_{0}
.
Therefore,
M_{bad},\sigma^{\prime}\nvDash\langle\!\!\langle a_{0}.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle
.

H.3.3.
M_{bad}\nvdash S_{2}

In order to prove that
M_{bad}\vdash S_{2}
, we would have needed to prove, among other things, that
set
satisfied
S_{2}
, which means proving that

\displaystyle\text{(ERR\_1?)}\ \ \ \ M_{bad}\ \vdash\

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{this}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}},\mbox{{\texttt{k'}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Key}}},a:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle a.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\{\mbox{{\texttt{this}}},\mbox{{\texttt{k'}}}\}\ \}

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt\mbox{{\texttt{this.key:=k'}}};

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt\mbox{{\texttt{res}}}:=0

\displaystyle\{\ \langle\!\!\langle a.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle a.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{res}}}\ \}\ \ \ ||\ \ \ \{...\}

However, we cannot establish
(ERR_1?)
.
Namely, when we take the case where
\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}=a
, we would need to establish, that

\displaystyle\text{(ERR\_2?)}\ \ \ \ M_{bad}\ \vdash\

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{this}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}},\mbox{{\texttt{k'}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Key}}}\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{this.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\{\mbox{{\texttt{this}}},\mbox{{\texttt{k'}}}\}\ \}

this.key:=k’

\displaystyle\{\ \langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\ \}\ \ \ ||\ \ \ \{...\}

And there is no way to prove
(ERR_2?)
. In fact,
(ERR_2?)
is not sound, for the reasons outlined in §
H.3.2
.

H.3.4.
M_{bad}
does not satisfy
S_{3}

We have already argued in Examples
2.3
and
6.5
that
M_{bad}
does not satisfy
S_{3}
, by giving a semantic argument – ie we are in state where
\langle\!\!\langle a_{0}.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle
, and execute
\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}_{0}.\mbox{{\texttt{set(k1)}}};\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}_{0}.\mbox{{\texttt{transfer}}}(...\mbox{{\texttt{k1}}})
.

Moreover, if we attempted to prove that
set
satisfies
S_{3}
, we would have to show that

\displaystyle\text{(ERR\_3?)}\ \ \ \ M_{bad}\ \vdash\

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{this}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}},\ \mbox{{\texttt{k'}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Key}}},a:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}},\,b:\mbox{{\texttt{int}}}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\ \ \ \langle\!\!\langle a.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\{\mbox{{\texttt{this}}},\mbox{{\texttt{k'}}}\}\ \wedge\ a.\mbox{{\texttt{}}}\geq b\ \}

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt\mbox{{\texttt{this.key:=k'}}};

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt~\hskip 25.6073pt\mbox{{\texttt{res}}}:=0

\displaystyle\{\ \langle\!\!\langle a.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle a.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{res}}}\ \wedge\ a.\mbox{{\texttt{}}}\geq b\ \}\ \ \ ||\ \ \ \{...\}

which, in the case of
a=\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}
would imply that

\displaystyle\text{(ERR\_4?)}\ \ \ \ M_{bad}\ \vdash\

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{this}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}},\ \mbox{{\texttt{k'}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Key}}},\,b:\mbox{{\texttt{int}}}\ \wedge

\displaystyle\ \ \ \langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\{\mbox{{\texttt{this}}},\mbox{{\texttt{k'}}}\}\ \wedge\ \mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.\mbox{{\texttt{}}}\geq b\ \}

this.key:=k’

\displaystyle\{\ \langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\ \}\ \ \ ||\ \ \ \{...\}

And
(ERR_4?)
cannot be proven and does not hold.

H.4.
Demonstrating that

, and that

As in §

, we redefine

. and have:

Lemma H.6 (module

Proof Outline

In order to prove that

.

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{A}}}_{trns},\,\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}\,,b:\mbox{{\texttt{int}}}\,\wedge\,{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{Ids}}}_{trns}\ \wedge\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\ \}

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073pt\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}::\mbox{{\texttt{transfer}}}.\mbox{{\texttt{body}}}\

\displaystyle\{{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\ \wedge\ {\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\mbox{{\texttt{res}}}}\wedge\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\ \}\ \ \ ||\ \ \ \{{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\wedge\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\ \}

\displaystyle\{{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\wedge\ {\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\mbox{{\texttt{res}}}}\wedge\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\ \}\ \ \ ||\ \ \ \{{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\wedge\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\ \}

where we are using ? to indicate that this needs to be proven, and

Lemma H.8 (function

satisfies
