).

\displaystyle\text{(32)}\ \ \ \ M_{good}\vdash\

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{A}}}_{trns},\,\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}\,,b:\mbox{{\texttt{int}}}\,\wedge\,{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{Ids}}}_{trns}\ \wedge\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\ \}

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073pt\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}::\mbox{{\texttt{transfer}}}.\mbox{{\texttt{body}}}\

\displaystyle\{{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\ \wedge\ {\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\mbox{{\texttt{res}}}}\wedge\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\ \}\ \ \ ||\ \ \ \{{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\wedge\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\ \}

Proof Outline

We will use the shorthand
stmts_{28-33}
for the statements in the body of
transfer
.
We will prove the preservation of protection, separately from the balance not decreasing when the key is protcted.

For the former, applying the steps in the proof of Lemma
H.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\text{(21)}\ \ \ \ M_{good}\vdash\

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{A}}}_{trns},\,\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}\,\wedge\,{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\,\wedge\,\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{Ids}}}_{trns}\ \}

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073ptstmts_{28-33}

\displaystyle\{{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\ \wedge\ {\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}\mbox{{\texttt{res}}}}\}\ \ \ ||\ \ \ \{{\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle}\}

For the latter, we rely on the underlying Hoare logic to ensure that no balance decreases, except perhaps that of the receiver, in which case its key was not protected.
Namely, we have that

\displaystyle\text{(71)}\ \ \ \ M_{good}\vdash_{u}l\

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{A}}}_{trns},\,\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}\,\wedge\,\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}=b\ \wedge\ (\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}\neq\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}\vee prg{this}.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\neq\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}^{\prime})\ \}

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073ptstmts_{28-33}

\displaystyle\{\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\}

We apply rules
Embed_UL
and
Mid
on (71), and obtain

\displaystyle\text{(72)}\ \ \ \ M_{good}\vdash\

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{A}}}_{trns},\,\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}\,\wedge\,\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}=b\ \wedge\ (\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}\neq\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}\vee prg{this}.\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}\neq\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}^{\prime})\ \}

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073ptstmts_{28-33}

\displaystyle\{\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\}\ \ \ ||\ \ \ \{\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\}

Moreover, we have

\begin{array}[]{llll}\text{(73)}&M_{good}&\vdash&\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{Ids}}}_{trns}\ \ \rightarrow\ \ \langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{key'}}}\\
\text{(74)}&M_{good}&\vdash&\langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{key'}}}\ \ \rightarrow\ \ \mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\neq\mbox{{\texttt{key'}}}\\
\text{(75)}&M_{good}&\vdash&\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\neq\mbox{{\texttt{key'}}}\ \ \rightarrow\ \ \mbox{{\texttt{a}}}\neq\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}\vee\mbox{{\texttt{this.key}}}\neq\mbox{{\texttt{key}}}^{\prime}\end{array}

normalsize

Applying (73), (74), (75) and
Conseq
on (72) we obtain:

\displaystyle\text{(76)}\ \ \ \ M_{good}\vdash\

\displaystyle\{\ \mbox{{\texttt{A}}}_{trns},\,\mbox{{\texttt{a}}}:\mbox{{\texttt{Account}}}\,\wedge\,\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}=b\ \wedge\ \langle\!\!\langle\mbox{{\texttt{a.key}}}\rangle\!\!\rangle\!\!\leftarrow\!\!\!\!\--\!\!\!\!\!\!\times\ \ \!\!\mbox{{\texttt{Ids}}}_{trns}\ \ \}

\displaystyle~\hskip 25.6073ptstmts_{28-33}

\displaystyle\{\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\}\ \ \ ||\ \ \ \{\mbox{{\texttt{a.}}}\geq b\}

We combine (72) and (76) through
Combine
and obtain (32).

\square

H.6.
Dealing with polymorphic function calls

The case split rules together with the rule of consequence allow our Hoare logic to formally reason about polymorphic calls, where the receiver may be internal or external.

We demonstrate this through an example where we may have an external receiver, or a receiver from a class
C
. Assume we had a module
M
with a scoped invariant (as in A), and an internal method specification as in (B).

\begin{array}[]{lclcl}&(A)&M&\vdash&\ \rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{{$\mathbb{A}\!\!$}}{y_{1}:D}.{{\{}}{A}{{\}}}\\
&(B)&M&\vdash&\{\,A_{1}\,\}\,\mbox{{\texttt{p}}}\ C\!::\!m(y_{1}:D)\,\{\,A_{2}\,\}{\parallel\{A_{3}\}}\\
\\
\end{array}

Here
p
may be
private
or
ublic
; the argument apples either way.

Assume also implications as in (C)-(H)

\begin{array}[]{lclcl}&(C)&M&\vdash&A_{0}\ \rightarrow\ A\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(y_{0},y_{1})\\
&(D)&M&\vdash&A\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(y_{0},y_{1})\rightarrow A_{4}\\
&(E)&M&\vdash&A\rightarrow A_{5}\\
&(F)&M&\vdash&A_{0}\rightarrow A_{1}[y_{0}/\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}]\\
&(G)&M&\vdash&A_{2}[y_{0},u/\mbox{{\texttt{this}}},res]\rightarrow A_{4}\\
&(H)&M&\vdash&A_{3}\rightarrow A_{5}\\
\end{array}

Then, by application of
Call_Ext_Adapt
on (A) we obtain (I)

~\hskip 25.6073pt(I)\ \ {M\ \vdash\ \{\,\ y_{0}:external,y_{1}:D\wedge{A\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(y_{0},y_{1})}\ \,\}\ u:=y_{0}.m(y_{1})\ \{\,\ A\!-\!\!\!\,\!{\triangledown}(y_{0},y_{1})\ \,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,\ A\ \,\}}\\

By application of the rule of consequence on (I) and (C), (D), and (E), we obtain

~\hskip 25.6073pt(J)\ \ {M\ \vdash\ \{\,\ y_{0}:external,y_{1}:D\wedge A_{0}\ \,\}\ u:=y_{0}.m(y_{1})\ \{\,\ A_{4}\ \,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,\ A_{5}\ \,\}}\\

Then, by application of
[Call_Intl]
on (B) we obtain (K)

~\hskip 25.6073pt(K)\ \ {M\ \vdash\ \{\,\ y_{0}:C,y_{1}:D\wedge A_{1}[y_{0}/\mbox{{\texttt{this}}}]\ \,\}\ u:=y_{0}.m(y_{1})\ \{\,\ A_{2}[y_{0},u/\mbox{{\texttt{this}}},res]\ \,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,\ A_{3}\ \,\}}\\

By application of the rule of consequence on (K) and (F), (G), and (H), we obtain

~\hskip 25.6073pt(L)\ \ {M\ \vdash\ \{\,\ y_{0}:C,y_{1}:D\wedge A_{0}\ \,\}\ u:=y_{0}.m(y_{1})\ \{\,\ A_{4}\ \,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,\ A_{5}\ \,\}}\\

By case split,
[Cases]
, on (J) and (L), we obtain

~\hskip 25.6073pt(polymoprhic)\ \ {M\ \vdash\ \{\,\ (y_{0}:external\vee y_{0}:C),y_{1}:D\wedge A_{0}\ \,\}\ u:=y_{0}.m(y_{1})\ \{\,\ A_{4}\ \,\}\,\,\parallel\,\,\{\,\ A_{5}\ \,\}}\\
